\pi^{-}_{L}:\operatorname{H}_{*}(U^{-}_{\eta,L})\oplus\operatorname{H}_{*}(U^{+}_{\eta,L})\to\operatorname{H}_{*}(U^{-}_{\eta,L})

is the projection.

Claim
(2): Let
H_{\eta}\leq H_{\nu}
. Let
L_{\eta}\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L_{\nu}\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
where
L_{\eta}\leq L_{\nu}
. We have that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\nu}}<L_{\nu}\}\cap\mathbb{R}_{+}\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\nu}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\nu}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\}\cap\mathbb{R}_{+}\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\eta}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\eta}\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{L_{\nu}}^{\eta,C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{L_{\eta}}^{\eta,C}}

commutes, where the vertical maps are inclusions. Also, we have that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\nu}}<L_{\nu}\}\cap\mathbb{R}_{+}\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\nu}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\}\cap\mathbb{R}_{+}\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\nu}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\nu}}<L_{\nu}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\}\cap\mathbb{R}_{+}\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\eta}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}\cap\mathbb{R}_{+}\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\eta}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\eta,C}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\eta,C}_{L_{\eta}}}

commutes, where the vertical maps are inclusions. Due to the commutativity of these two diagrams, the fact that
i^{\eta,C}_{L_{\eta}}
and
i^{\eta,C}_{L_{\nu}}
induce isomorphisms in homology, and the functoriality property of long exact sequences for topological pairs, we have that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{\nu}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(U^{-}_{\nu,L_{\nu}})\oplus\operatorname{H}_{*}(U^{+}_{\nu,L_{\nu}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\nu}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(U^{-}_{\eta,L_{\eta}})\oplus\operatorname{H}_{*}(U^{+}_{\eta,L_{\eta}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\eta}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\partial}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(N_{\varepsilon}^{\nu,C})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\partial}_{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H,\varepsilon}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(N_{\varepsilon}^{\eta,C})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{C}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}

commutes. Since from claim (3) of Lemma
9.13
we have that
(i^{H,\varepsilon}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}
preserves splitting, it follows from claim (2) Lemma
9.15
that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\nu}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*+1}(\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}^{*}}<L_{\nu}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}^{*}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{C}<L_{\eta}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*+1}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}^{*}}<L_{\eta}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}^{*}}<\varepsilon\})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H^{*}_{\nu}}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{C}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}

commutes.
∎

10  Positive (
S^{1}
-equivariant) symplectic homology and
autonomous Hamiltonians

In this section, we compare the positive (
S^{1}
-equivariant) symplectic homology of the convex domain with the singular (
S^{1}
-equivariant) homology of sublevel sets of a dual functional of the form

\Psi_{H^{*}}(x)=-\frac{1}{2}\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}}\langle J_{0}x(t),\dot{x}(t)\rangle\,dt+\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}}H^{*}(-J_{0}\dot{x})\,dt,

for particular types of Hamiltonians.

Let
C\subset\mathbb{R}^{2n}
be a non-degenerate, strongly convex domain whose interior contains the origin (see Section
9
for the definitions of these properties).

Admissible approximating functions:

A smooth function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{R}
belongs to
\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
if it satisfies:

(1)

For some
0<s_{\varepsilon}<1
,
\varphi(s)=\delta s-\zeta_{\varepsilon}
for
s\in[0,s_{\varepsilon}]
, where
0<\zeta_{\varepsilon}<\varepsilon
and
0<\delta<\frac{\varepsilon}{4}
.

(2)

For some
s_{\eta}>1
,
\varphi(s)=\eta s-\zeta_{\eta}
for
s\in[s_{\eta},+\infty)
, where
\eta\notin\sigma(\partial C)
and
\eta>\min\sigma(\partial C)
.

(3)

\varphi^{\prime\prime}(s)>0
for
s\in(s_{\varepsilon},s_{\eta})
, and
\varphi(1)<0
and
\varphi^{\prime}(1)<\min\sigma(\partial C)
.

(4)

For
s_{\max}\in(s_{\varepsilon},s_{\eta})
such that
\varphi^{\prime}(s_{\max})=T_{\max}=\max\sigma(\partial C)^{<\eta}
, it holds that

\varphi^{\prime}(s_{\max})s_{\max}-\varphi(s_{\max})<\eta

and

\varphi^{\prime}(s_{\max})(s_{\max}-1)-\varphi(s_{\max})<\min\left\{\frac{1}{\eta},\frac{1}{3}\text{gap}(\eta)\right\},

where
\text{gap}(\eta)
is the minimal distance between two elements in
\sigma(\partial C)^{<\eta}
.

We will emphasize the slope
\eta
of
\varphi
by writing
\varphi_{\eta}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\varphi
.

Remark 10.1
.

We claim that for
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
, the inequality

(10.1)

\varphi^{\prime}(s)s-\varphi(s)>\varphi^{\prime}(s),\quad s>1

holds. Indeed, from (2) and (3), we have that
g(s)=\varphi^{\prime}(s)(s-1)-\varphi(s)
is a non-decreasing function for
s>1
. On the other hand, from (3), we have that
g(1)>0
. Therefore, (
10.1
) holds. From (3),
\varphi^{\prime}:(s_{\varepsilon},s_{\eta})\to(\delta,\eta)
is an increasing bijection. Thus,
s_{\max}
is well-defined and unique. Additionally,
\varphi^{\prime}(1)<\min\sigma(\partial C)
, which implies that there exists a unique
s_{\min}>1
such that
\varphi^{\prime}(s_{\min})=T_{\min}=\min\sigma(\partial C)^{<\eta}=\min\sigma(\partial C)
. From (
10.1
), it follows that
\varphi^{\prime}(s_{\min})s_{\min}-\varphi(s_{\min})>T_{\min}=2\varepsilon
. Using this conclusion, the fact that
\varphi^{\prime}(s)s-\varphi(s)
is increasing, and (4), we conclude that

(10.2)

2\varepsilon<\varphi^{\prime}(s)s-\varphi(s)<\eta,\quad s\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]

Therefore,
\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)\subset\mathcal{F}^{*}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
.

Moreover, the increasing property of the function
g(s)
, its positivity for
s>1
, and (4) imply that

(10.3)

|\varphi^{\prime}(s)(s-1)-\varphi(s)|<\min\left\{\frac{1}{\eta},\frac{1}{3}\text{gap}(\eta)\right\},\quad s\in[s_{\min},s_{\max}]

Now, we state the main Proposition of this section.

Proposition 10.2
.

Let
C
be a non-degenerate strongly convex domain whose interior contains the origin. Let
\varphi_{\eta}\in\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
and let
T_{\eta}
be the minimal element of
\sigma(\partial C)
greater than
\eta
, and
H_{\eta}=\varphi_{\eta}\circ H_{C}
. The following holds:

(1.1)

For every
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
, there exists an isomorphism

D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}:\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\to\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2}).

(1.2)

This isomorphism is natural. Let
H_{\eta}\leq H_{\nu}
. Then, for every
L_{\eta}\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L_{\nu}\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
such that
L_{\eta}\leq L_{\nu}
, the following diagram commutes:

\hbox to262.06pt{\vbox to58.96pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 131.02605pt\lower-27.91779pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Remark 10.3
.

The same claim holds if one replaces singular homology with
S^{1}
-equivariant homology and positive symplectic homology with positive
S^{1}
-equivariant symplectic homology. Unlike in the previous section, the proof of the
S^{1}
-equivariant case differs. We will emphasize these differences throughout the section.

For definitions of (
S^{1}
-equivariant) symplectic homology and their positive versions, we refer the reader to
[
Vit99
,
BO17
,
GH18
]
.

We define another family with
F_{\infty}(C)
of smooth functions
\varphi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
such that

(1)

There exists
\varphi_{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
such that
\varphi|_{[1,+\infty)}=\varphi_{\nu}|_{[1,+\infty)}
.

(2)

\varphi
is increasing on
(0,+\infty)
and
\varphi^{\prime\prime}(s)>0
for
s\in(0,s_{\eta})
.

(3)

\varphi(s)=\varphi(0)>\varepsilon
for
s\leq 0
.

Notice that both families are non-empty for every
\eta\notin\sigma(\partial C)
.

Now, we define a family of admissible Morse-Bott Hamiltonians with respect to the family
\mathcal{F}_{\infty}(C)
(denoted
\mathcal{H}_{MB}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
) as the family of smooth Hamiltonians
H:\mathbb{R}^{2n}\to\mathbb{R}
of the form

H=\varphi\circ H_{C}+f,

where
\varphi\in F_{\infty}(C)
and
f:\mathbb{R}^{2n}\to\mathbb{R}
is a
C^{2}
-small function such that it is supported inside
C
, and its second derivative is positive definite near the origin. Additionally, we require that
H
is quadratically convex,
H|_{\overline{C}}<0
, and
H(0)>-\varepsilon
.

Remark 10.4
.

Notice that
\mathcal{H}_{MB}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
is a cofinal subset of
\mathcal{H}_{MB}(C)
defined in
[
GH18
]
. Moreover,
H\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}_{MB}(C)
has a unique trivial critical orbit of
\Phi_{H}
at
0
and it holds that

\Phi_{H}(0)<\varepsilon.

Additionally, for non-trivial critical orbits
S_{y}
of
\Phi_{H}
, from (
10.2
), we have that

2\varepsilon<\Phi_{H}(y)<\eta.

Moreover, from (
10.3
), we have that

|\Phi_{H}(y)-T_{y}|<\min\left\{\frac{1}{\eta},\frac{1}{3}\text{gap}(\eta)\right\},

where
T_{y}
is the period of the corresponding closed Reeb orbit, which proves that
\mathcal{H}_{MB}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{MB}(C)
.

Positive Symplectic homology

As described in
[
CFHW96
]
and
[
BO09
]
, we can perturb
H\in\mathcal{H}_{MB}(C)
to a time-dependent Hamiltonian of the form

H^{\prime}(t,x)=H(x)+\delta\sum_{S_{y}}\rho_{S_{y}}(x)f_{S_{y}}(t,x),

where the summation is taken over all non-trivial critical orbits. The functions
\rho_{S_{y}}
and
f_{S_{y}}
are defined locally near the given critical orbit, with
\rho_{S_{y}}
serving as a support function. Moreover, the following claim holds.

Lemma 10.5
.

For
H\in\mathcal{H}_{MB}(C)
and a small enough
\delta>0
, a time-dependent perturbation
H^{\prime}
of
H
satisfies the following:

(1)

The constant orbits of
H^{\prime}
are the same as those of
H
.

(2)

For every non-trivial critical orbit
S_{y}
of
H
, there exists a pair of non-degenerate critical orbits of
H^{\prime}
, denoted
(y_{\min},y_{\max})
, which belong to
S_{y}
and satisfy

\mu_{CZ}(y_{\min})=\mu_{CZ}(y),\quad\text{and}\quad\mu_{CZ}(y_{\max})=\mu_{CZ}(y)+1.

The proof of this lemma can be found in
[
CFHW96
]
and
[
BO09
]
.

Perturbations of this type appear in Morse theory for functions of Morse-Bott type (see
[
BH09
,
BH13
]
). Instead of working with the Floer complex of perturbed Morse-Bott Hamiltonians, one can work with cascades and Morse-Bott moduli spaces (see
[
BO09
]
). These two approaches are equivalent. Similar results can be obtained in the Morse case, as shown in
[
BH13
]
.

A Hamiltonian
H^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
is an admissible perturbation of
H\in\mathcal{H}_{MB}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
if

H^{\prime}(t,x)=H(x)+\delta\sum_{S_{y}}\rho_{S_{y}}(x)f_{S_{y}}(t,x),

and the following conditions hold:

•

Conditions (1) and (2) of Lemma
10.5
are satisfied.

•

The time-dependent perturbations are supported outside of
\overline{C}
.

•

H^{\prime}
is quadratically convex.

•

For the pair
(y_{\min},y_{\max})
corresponding to
S_{y}
, it holds that

|\Phi_{H^{\prime}}(y_{\min})-T_{y}|<\min\left\{\frac{1}{\eta},\frac{1}{3}\text{gap}(\eta)\right\},\quad\text{and}\quad|\Phi_{H^{\prime}}(y_{\max})-T_{y}|<\min\left\{\frac{1}{\eta},\frac{1}{3}\text{gap}(\eta)\right\}.

•

For every
y\in\operatorname{crit}(\Phi_{H})\setminus\{0\}
, it holds that

\varepsilon<\Phi_{H}(y)<\eta.

For
H\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
, we define the positive Floer complex as

\{\operatorname{C}\operatorname{F}_{*}^{+}(H),\partial^{F}\},

where

\operatorname{C}\operatorname{F}^{+}(H)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\operatorname{C}\operatorname{F}(H)/\operatorname{C}\operatorname{F}^{<\varepsilon}(H).

This is well-defined since
\Phi_{H}
is non-increasing along trajectories. The filtration on this complex by
\Phi_{H}
is well-defined for the same reason. The homology of this complex is denoted by

\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}^{+}_{*}(H,J)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\operatorname{C}\operatorname{F}_{*}^{+}(H),\partial^{F}\}).

This homology is independent of the almost complex structure.

If
(H_{\alpha},J_{\alpha})
and
(H_{\beta},J_{\beta})
are such that
H_{\alpha},H_{\beta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
and
H_{\alpha}\leq H_{\beta}
, then a generic non-increasing homotopy
(H^{\alpha\beta}_{s},J^{\alpha\beta}_{s})
induces a chain map described in Section
1
. This chain map induces a continuation map

i^{+}_{H_{\beta},H_{\alpha}}:\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}^{+}_{*}(H_{\alpha},J_{\alpha})\to\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}^{+}_{*}(H_{\beta},J_{\beta}),

which gives us a directed system. Here,
\alpha
and
\beta
do not represent the slope.

The set
\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
is cofinal in the set of admissible non-degenerate Hamiltonians (see
[
GH18
]
). Therefore, we define the filtered positive symplectic homology as

SH^{+,<L}(C)=\varinjlim_{H\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)}HF^{+,<L}(H).

Remark 10.6
.

Since
\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}
, for
H
its corresponding
l
-reduced dual functional is
\psi^{l}_{H^{*}}
. We analogously define the positive Morse complex as the quotient complex

\operatorname{C}\operatorname{M}^{+}(\psi^{l}_{H^{*}})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\operatorname{C}\operatorname{M}(\psi^{l}_{H^{*}})/\operatorname{C}\operatorname{M}^{<\varepsilon}(\psi^{l}_{H^{*}}).

The claim (1) of Theorem
8.1
and the fact that corresponding critical points of functionals have the same action imply that the map induced from
\Theta
in positive homology, denoted

\Theta^{+}_{\operatorname{H}}:\operatorname{H}\operatorname{M}_{*}^{+}(\psi^{l}_{H^{*}},g)\to\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}_{*+n}^{+}(H,J),

is an isomorphism that preserves action filtration.

Let
H_{\alpha}\leq H_{\beta}
and
H_{\alpha},H_{\beta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
. Assuming that the
l
-saddle point reduction exists for both dual functionals, we have from claim (2) of Theorem
8.2
that the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{M}_{*}^{+}(\psi^{l}_{H_{\beta}^{*}},g_{\beta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}_{*+n}^{+}(H_{\beta},J_{\beta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{M}_{*}^{+}(\psi^{l}_{H_{\alpha}^{*}},g_{\alpha})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}_{*+n}^{+}(H_{\alpha},J_{\alpha})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta^{+}_{\beta,\operatorname{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta^{+}_{\alpha,\operatorname{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{M,+}_{H_{\beta}^{*},H_{\alpha}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{F,+}_{H_{\beta},H_{\alpha}}}

commutes, where vertical maps are continuation maps.

Lemma 10.7
.

Let
C
be a non-degenerate, strongly convex domain whose interior contains the origin. Let
H_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
be arbitrary, where
\eta
is the slope at infinity and
T_{\eta}
is the minimal element of
\sigma(\partial C)
greater than
\eta
. The following holds:

(1)

For every
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
, there exists an isomorphism

D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}:\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\to SH^{+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2}).

(2)

This isomorphism is natural. Let
H_{\eta}\leq H_{\nu}
. For every
L_{\eta}\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L_{\nu}\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
such that
L_{\eta}\leq L_{\nu}
, the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<L_{\nu}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L_{\nu}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L_{\eta}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\nu}}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta}}_{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}

commutes.

Proof.

Let
H_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
be arbitrary, and let
J
be generically chosen. Since the slope of
H_{\eta}
is
\eta
, it follows that the inclusion

\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}^{+}_{*}(H_{\eta},J)\to\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{+,<\eta}_{*}(C)

is an isomorphism. Moreover, for every critical point
y\in\text{crit}(\Phi_{H^{\prime}_{\eta}})
, we have

\Phi_{H_{\eta}}(y)<\eta.

Combining this with the fact that there are no elements of
\sigma(\partial C)
inside
[\nu,T_{\nu})
, we conclude that

i^{\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{+},H_{\eta}}_{L}:\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}^{+,<L}_{*}(H_{\eta})\to\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{+,<L}_{*}(C),\quad L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})

is an isomorphism.

Let
\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}}
be the
l
-reduced dual functional. For a generic
J
and
g\in\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{H}_{l})
uniformly equivalent to the standard metric, we have, by Remark
10.6
, that

\theta^{+,<L}_{\operatorname{H}}:\operatorname{H}\operatorname{M}^{+<L}_{*}(\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}},g)\to\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}_{*+n}^{+,<L}(H_{\eta},J),\quad L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})

is an isomorphism. Additionally, by cellular filtration of the manifold, we have a natural isomorphism

h^{L}:\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to\operatorname{H}\operatorname{M}^{+<L}_{*}(\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}},g),\quad L\in(\eta,T_{\eta}).

Thus, for
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
, we have the map

D^{H_{\eta},l}_{L}:\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to SH^{+,<L}_{*+n}(C),\quad D^{H_{\eta},l}=i^{\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{+},H_{\eta}}_{L}\circ\theta^{+,<L}_{\operatorname{H}}\circ h^{L},

which is an isomorphism.

For
H_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
, the map
D^{H_{\eta},l}_{L}
is defined for sufficiently large
l\in\mathbb{N}
, since the
l
-reduced dual functionals always exists if
l
is large enough.

This map does not depend on the choice of
J
and
g
. Indeed, let
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
be two different almost complex structures, and let
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
be two different metrics on
\mathbb{H}_{l}
uniformly equivalent to the standard one. Then, the following diagrams

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{62.38824pt}{-2.24791pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 86.33 -3.11)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{M}_{*}^{+,<L}(\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}^{*}},g_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{M}_{*}^{+,<L}(\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}^{*}},g_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{L,g_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{L,g_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{M,+}_{L}}

commute. The second diagram is precisely the diagram from Remark
10.6
because all critical points have action smaller than
\eta
and
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
. Combining these three diagrams, we see that
D^{H_{\eta},l}
does not depend on the choice of
J
and
g
.

Let
H_{\eta},H_{\nu}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
be such that
H_{\eta}\leq H_{\nu}
. Let
l
be large enough such that
D^{H_{\eta},l}
and
D^{H_{\nu},l}
are defined. Then, for every
L_{\eta}\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L_{\nu}\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
, we have the following commutative diagram:

(10.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\nu}}<L_{\nu}\},\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+<L_{\nu}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\},\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+<L_{\eta}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\nu},l}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta},l}_{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H,l}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}

commutes, where
i^{H,l}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}
is the inclusion of the corresponding pairs. This commutation follows from the diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}_{*}^{+,<L_{\nu}}(H_{\nu},J_{\nu})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{+,<L_{\nu}}_{*}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}_{*}^{+,<L_{\eta}}(H_{\eta},J_{\eta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{+<L_{\eta}}_{*}(C)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{+},H_{\nu}}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{+},H_{\eta}}_{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{F,+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{M}_{*}^{+,<L_{\nu}}(\psi^{l}_{H_{\nu}^{*}},g_{\nu})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}_{*+n}^{+,<L_{\nu}}(H_{\nu},J_{\nu})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{M}_{*}^{+,<L_{\eta}}(\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}^{*}},g_{\eta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}_{*+n}^{+,<L_{\eta}}(H_{\eta},J_{\eta})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta^{+,<L_{\nu}}_{\nu,\operatorname{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta^{+,<L_{\eta}}_{\eta,\operatorname{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{M,+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{F,+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\nu}^{*}}<L_{\nu}\},\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\nu}^{*}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{M}_{*}^{+,<L_{\nu}}(\psi^{l}_{H_{\nu}^{*}},g_{\nu})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}<L_{\eta}\},\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}\operatorname{M}_{*}^{+,<L_{\eta}}(\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}^{*}},g_{\eta})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H,l}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{M,+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}

The second diagram is, as in the previous case, a diagram from Remark
10.6
.

Now, let
H_{\eta}
be a fixed Hamiltonian from the set
\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
. We define

D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}:\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\to SH^{+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2}),\quad L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})

as

D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}=D^{H_{\eta},l}_{L}\circ(\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l})_{*},

where
l\in\mathbb{N}
is any number for which the
l
-reduced dual functional associated with
H_{\eta}
exists, and

\mathbb{P}_{l}^{L}:(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to(\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}),

is the restriction of the projection
\mathbb{P}_{l}
. The map
\mathbb{P}_{l}^{L}
is well-defined and is a homotopy equivalence. Indeed, we have that

\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}(x)=\Psi_{H_{\eta}^{*}}(\Gamma_{l}(x)),

where

\Gamma_{l}:\mathbb{H}_{l}\to H^{1}_{0}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{R}^{2n}),\quad\Gamma_{l}(x)=(x,Y_{l}(x)),

and
Y_{l}(x)
is the unique global minimizer of
\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}
on
\{x\}\times\mathbb{H}_{l}
. Therefore,
\mathbb{P}_{l}^{L}
is well-defined. On the other hand,
\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}
is convex on
\{x\}\times\mathbb{H}^{l}
. Combining this with the fact that
Y_{l}(x)
is the unique global minimizer, we conclude that
\mathbb{P}_{l}^{L}
is a homotopy inverse of

\Gamma_{l}^{L}:(\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}).

Therefore,
\mathbb{P}_{l}^{L}
is a homotopy equivalence and induces an isomorphism in homology.

Now, we need to show that the map
D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}
is well-defined, i.e., that it does not depend on
l
.

Let
l_{1},l_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
be two different numbers for which the
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
-reduced dual functionals associated with
H_{\eta}
exist. We can assume that
l_{1}<l_{2}
. We have a natural orthogonal splitting of
\mathbb{H}_{l_{2}}
given by

\mathbb{H}_{l_{2}}=\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}}\oplus\mathbb{H}^{l_{2},l_{1}},

where

\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}}=\{x\in{H^{1}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{R}^{2n})}\mid\hat{x}(k)=0\text{ if }k<1\text{ or }k>l_{1}\},

and

\mathbb{H}^{l_{2},l_{1}}=\{x\in{H^{1}(\mathbb{T},\mathbb{R}^{2n})}\mid\hat{x}(k)=0\text{ if }k<l_{1}+1\text{ or }k>l_{2}\}.

We use
x
as a coordinate for
\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}}
and
y
as a coordinate for
\mathbb{H}^{l_{2},l_{1}}
. With

\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}:\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}}\to\mathbb{H}_{l_{2}},\quad\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}=\mathbb{P}_{l_{2}}\circ\Gamma_{l_{1}}=(x,\mathbb{P}_{l_{2}}\circ Y_{l_{1}}(x)),

which is the graph of
\mathbb{P}_{l_{2}}\circ Y_{l_{1}}
. We want to show that the following diagram

(10.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta},l_{2}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta},l_{1}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Gamma^{L}_{l_{2},l_{1}})_{*}}

commutes, where

\Gamma^{L}_{l_{2},l_{1}}:(\{\psi^{l_{1}}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l_{1}}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to(\{\psi^{l_{2}}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l_{2}}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}),

is a homotopy equivalence, with homotopy inverse being the map

\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l_{1},l_{2}}:(\{\psi^{l_{2}}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l_{2}}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to(\{\psi^{l_{1}}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\psi^{l_{1}}_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}),

which is the restriction of
\mathbb{P}_{l_{1}}
.

For a fixed
x\in\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}}
, the map

\mathbb{H}^{l_{2},l_{1}}\to\mathbb{R},\quad y\mapsto\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}(x+y),

has a unique minimizer, which is precisely
\mathbb{P}_{l_{2}}\circ Y_{l_{1}}(x)\in\mathbb{H}^{l_{2},l_{1}}
. Additionally,
\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}
is convex on
\{x\}\times\mathbb{H}^{l_{2},l_{1}}
. Therefore, as before, we have that
\Gamma^{L}_{l_{2},l_{1}}
and
\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l_{1},l_{2}}
are such that

(10.6)

\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l_{1},l_{2}}\circ\Gamma^{L}_{l_{2},l_{1}}=\text{id},\quad\Gamma^{L}_{l_{2},l_{1}}\circ\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l_{1},l_{2}}\simeq\text{id}

holds. From the previous discussion, it follows that

(10.7)

\frac{\partial\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}}{\partial y}(\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(x))=0,\quad x\in\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}}

On the other hand,
\mathbb{H}^{l_{2},l_{1}}
is transverse to
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}})
because it is a graph. Therefore, we choose
g_{2}\in\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{H}_{l_{2}})
uniformly equivalent to the standard metric such that
\mathbb{H}^{l_{2},l_{1}}
is normal to
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}})
. For such a metric, it follows from (
10.7
) that
\nabla_{g_{2}}\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}
is tangent to
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}})
. We take
g_{1}\in\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}})
such that
g_{1}=(\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}})^{*}g_{2}
. This metric will be equivalent to the standard one because
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}
is bilipschitz. Let
g_{1}
,
g_{2}
, and an almost complex structure
J
be chosen such that the above requirements are met and the maps

\Theta_{1}:\operatorname{C}\operatorname{M}_{*}(\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}},g_{1})\to\operatorname{C}\operatorname{F}_{*+n}(H_{\eta},J)

and

\Theta_{2}:\operatorname{C}\operatorname{M}_{*}(\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}},g_{2})\to\operatorname{C}\operatorname{F}_{*+n}(H_{\eta},J)

are well-defined.

Let
x_{l_{1}}
be a critical point of
\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}
. Then
\Gamma_{l_{1},l_{2}}(x_{l_{1}})=x_{l_{2}}
is a critical point of
\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}
. Moreover, the map

(10.8)

\Gamma_{l_{1},l_{2}}|_{\operatorname{crit}(\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}})}:\operatorname{crit}(\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}})\to\operatorname{crit}(\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}})

is a bijection.

Being that
g_{1}=(\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}})^{*}g_{2}
and by properties of
g_{2}
, it follows that

(10.9)

\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(W^{u}(x_{l_{1}};-\nabla_{g_{1}}\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}))=W^{u}(x_{l_{2}};-\nabla_{g_{2}}\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}).

Indeed, since
g_{1}=(\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}})^{*}g_{2}
, we have that
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(W^{u}(x_{l_{1}};-\nabla_{g_{1}}\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}))
is the unstable manifold at
x_{l_{2}}
associated with the pair
(\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}|_{\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}})},g_{2}|_{\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}})})
. Since
\nabla_{g_{2}}\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}
is tangent to
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}})
and the map (
10.8
) is a bijection, we conclude that

\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(W^{u}(x_{l_{1}};-\nabla_{g_{1}}\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}))\subseteq W^{u}(x_{l_{2}};-\nabla_{g_{2}}\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}).

Additionally,
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(W^{u}(x_{l_{1}};-\nabla_{g_{1}}\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}))
and
W^{u}(x_{l_{2}};-\nabla_{g_{2}}\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}})
have the same dimension since

\operatorname{ind}(x_{l};\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}^{*}})=\mu_{CZ}(x;H_{\eta})-n

for every
l
for which
l
-saddle point reduction exists. Therefore,
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(W^{u}(x_{l_{1}};-\nabla_{g_{1}}\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}))
is an open connected subset of
W^{u}(x_{l_{2}};-\nabla_{g_{2}}\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}})
, which is also open and connected. On the other hand,
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(W^{u}(x_{l_{1}};-\nabla_{g_{1}}\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}))
is part of the graph
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(\mathbb{H}_{l_{1}})
, which is closed. Therefore, the unstable manifold
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(W^{u}(x_{l_{1}};-\nabla_{g_{1}}\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}))
is also closed in
W^{u}(x_{l_{2}};-\nabla_{g_{2}}\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}})
, which implies that (
10.9
) holds.

Therefore,
\Gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}
induces a chain isomorphism

\gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}:\operatorname{C}\operatorname{M}_{*}(\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}},g_{1})\to\operatorname{C}\operatorname{M}_{*}(\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}},g_{2})

given by

\gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}(x_{l_{1}})=x_{l_{2}}.

Moreover, (
10.9
) gives us that

\Gamma_{l_{1},+}(W^{u}(x_{l_{1}};-\nabla_{g_{1}}\psi^{l_{1}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}}))=\Gamma_{l_{2},+}(W^{u}(x_{l_{2}};-\nabla_{g_{2}}\psi^{l_{2}}_{H_{\eta}^{*}})).

Therefore,

\mathcal{M}_{\Theta}(x_{l_{1}},y;H_{\eta},J,g_{1})=\mathcal{M}_{\Theta}(x_{l_{2}},y;H_{\eta},J,g_{2}),

which implies that the following diagram

\hbox to184.55pt{\vbox to58pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 92.27205pt\lower-28.68144pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

the map induced from
\gamma_{l_{2},l_{1}}
in positive homology, we have that the diagram

\hbox to205.83pt{\vbox to60.63pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 102.91156pt\lower-28.68144pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

{{{{{}}{

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

we get that diagram (
10.5
) commutes. From there and (
10.6
), we get that

D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta},l_{2}}_{L}\circ(\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l_{2}})_{*}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta},l_{2}}_{L}\circ(id)_{*}\circ(\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l_{2}})_{*}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta},l_{2}}_{L}\circ(\Gamma^{L}_{l_{2},l_{1}}\circ\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l_{1},l_{2}})_{*}\circ(\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l_{2}})_{*}

=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta},l_{2}}_{L}\circ(\Gamma^{L}_{l_{2},l_{1}})_{*}\circ(\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l_{1},l_{2}}\circ\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l_{2}})_{*}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta},l_{1}}_{L}\circ(\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l_{1}})_{*}.

Thus, for
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
, the definition of
D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta},l}_{L}\circ(\mathbb{P}^{L}_{l})_{*}
is independent of
l
, i.e., it is well-defined. Moreover, it is an isomorphism since both maps are isomorphisms.

Let
H_{\eta},H_{\nu}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
such that
H_{\eta}\leq H_{\nu}
. Let
l\in\mathbb{N}
be large enough such that
l
-saddle point reduction exists for dual functionals associated with
H_{\eta}
and
H_{\nu}
.

For
L_{\eta}\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L_{\nu}\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
such that
L_{\eta}\leq L_{\nu}
, we have that the following diagram

(10.10)

\hbox to269.82pt{\vbox to58.74pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 134.90788pt\lower-28.19533pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

commutes.

We already know that diagram (
10.4
), i.e., the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\nu}}<L_{\nu}\},\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+<L_{\nu}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\},\{\psi^{l}_{H_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L_{\eta}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\nu},l}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta},l}_{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H,l}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}

commutes. Since
D^{H^{\prime}_{\nu}}_{L_{\eta}}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta},l}_{L_{\eta}}\circ(\mathbb{P}_{l}^{L_{\eta}})
and
D^{H^{\prime}_{\nu}}_{L_{\nu}}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\nu},l}_{L_{\nu}}\circ(\mathbb{P}_{l}^{L_{\nu}})
, combining the diagram induced in homology from diagram (
10.10
) and the commutativity of the last diagram, we conclude that the diagram of the claim (2) of this lemma commutes.

∎

Corollary 10.8
.

Let
C
be a non-degenerate, strongly convex domain whose interior contains the origin. Let
H_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}_{MB}(C)
be arbitrary, where
\eta
is the slope at infinity and
T_{\eta}
is the smallest element of
\sigma(\partial C)
greater than
\eta
. The following holds:

(1)

For every
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
, there exists an isomorphism

D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}:\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\to SH^{+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2}).

(2)

This isomorphism is natural. Let
H_{\eta}\leq H_{\nu}
. For every
L_{\eta}\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L_{\nu}\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
such that
L_{\eta}\leq L_{\nu}
, the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<L_{\nu}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L_{\nu}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L_{\eta}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\nu}}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta}}_{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}

commutes.

Proof.

Claim
(1):
Let
H_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}_{MB}(C)
be arbitrary, and let
\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C,H_{\eta})
denote the subset of perturbations
H_{\eta}^{\prime}
of
H_{\eta}
such that
H^{\prime}_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
and

•

H_{\eta}\leq H^{\prime}_{\eta}
.

From this, it follows that
\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}\leq\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}
, and therefore we have the inclusion

i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}:(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}),\quad L\in(\eta,T_{\eta}).

This inclusion defines an isomorphism in homology. Therefore, we define

D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}:\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\to SH^{+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2}),\quad L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})

as

D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}\circ(i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*},\quad H^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C,H_{\eta}).

First, we need to show that
i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}
indeed induces an isomorphism in homology.

Case 1:

H^{\prime}_{\eta}
is close to
H_{\eta}
.

Let
H^{\prime}_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C,H_{\eta})
be such that
H_{\eta}\geq H^{\prime}_{\eta}-\varepsilon^{\prime}
, where
\varepsilon^{\prime}<\min\left\{\varepsilon-\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}(0),\frac{T_{\eta}-\eta}{4}\right\}
. We will show that in this case
(i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*}
is an isomorphism for all
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
. Notice that it is enough to show that the previous inclusion is an isomorphism for one
L_{0}\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
. Indeed, if
L\in(L_{0},T_{\eta})
, we have from the fact that both
\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}
and
\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}
satisfy the PS condition and there are no critical values of either function in
[L_{0},L]
that inclusions

(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})

and

(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})

induce isomorphisms in homology. Now, from the commutativity of the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}}

and the fact that
i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L_{0}}
induces an isomorphism in homology, it follows that for all
L\in(L_{0},T_{\eta})
, the map
i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}
induces an isomorphism in homology. Similarly, we show that
i^{H^{*}_{\eta},H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}_{L}
induces an isomorphism in homology for all
L\in(\eta,L_{0})
.

Let
L_{0}\in(\eta,\frac{3T_{\eta}+\eta}{4})
. We will show that
i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L_{0}}
is injective in homology.

From the relation
H_{\eta}\leq H^{\prime}_{\eta}\leq H_{\eta}+\varepsilon^{\prime}
, it follows that
\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}-\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}\leq\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}
. We have the following factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}+\varepsilon^{\prime}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}\})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L_{0}}}

where all the maps are inclusions. We have that the inclusion

(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}+\varepsilon^{\prime}\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}\})

induces an isomorphism in homology, which implies from the previous diagram that
i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L_{0}}
is injective in homology.

Indeed, we have that the inclusion

\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\}\to\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}+\varepsilon^{\prime}\}

induces an isomorphism in homology since
L_{0}+\varepsilon^{\prime}<T_{\eta}
,

and the inclusion

\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}\to\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}\}

also induces an isomorphism since
\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}(0)<\varepsilon
,
\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}<2\varepsilon
, and

\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}(x)>2\varepsilon,\quad x\in\operatorname{crit}(\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}})\setminus\{0\}.

The last inequality follows from Remark
10.4
.

Therefore, by the five lemma, it follows that the inclusion

(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}+\varepsilon^{\prime}\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}\})

indeed induces an isomorphism in homology.

Let
L_{0}\in(\frac{3\eta+T_{\eta}}{4},T_{\eta})
. We will show that
i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L_{0}}
is surjective in homology.

From the relation
H^{\prime}_{\eta}-\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq H_{\eta}\leq H^{\prime}_{\eta}
, it follows that
\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}\leq\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}\leq\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}+\varepsilon^{\prime}
. We have the following factorization

\hbox to399.16pt{\vbox to50.18pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 199.57852pt\lower-25.08723pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

The inclusion

(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}-\varepsilon^{\prime}\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{\prime}\})\to(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})

induces an isomorphism in homology, which implies from the previous diagram that
i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L_{0}}
is surjective in homology. Indeed, since
L_{0}-\varepsilon^{\prime}>\eta
and
\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{\prime}>\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}(0)
(it holds that
\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}(0)=\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}(0)
), we have that the inclusions

\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}-\varepsilon^{\prime}\}\to\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\},\quad\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{\prime}\}\to\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\},

induce isomorphisms in homology, which implies that

(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}-\varepsilon^{\prime}\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon-\varepsilon^{\prime}\})\to(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L_{0}\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})

induces an isomorphism in homology. Hence, the map
i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L_{0}}
is surjective in homology.

Combining the previous conclusions, we have that
i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L_{0}}
induces an isomorphism in homology for
L_{0}\in(\frac{3\eta+T_{\eta}}{4},\frac{3T_{\eta}+\eta}{4})
. This implies, by the discussion at the beginning of the proof, that
i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}
induces an isomorphism in homology for every
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
.

Case 2:
Arbitrary
H^{\prime}_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},+}(C,H_{\eta})

Let
H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C,H_{\eta})
be such that
H^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}\leq H^{\prime}_{\eta}
and

(i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime*},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*},\quad L\in(\eta,T_{\eta}),

is an isomorphism.

Such an
H^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}
always exists due to the definition of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
and the previous case. Then we have a factorization

\hbox to386.23pt{\vbox to52.46pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 193.11055pt\lower-26.22842pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

On the other hand, from Lemma
10.7
, we have that

(i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*},\quad L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})

is an isomorphism. From the last diagram, we have

(i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*}=(i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta}}_{L}\circ i^{H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*}=(i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*}\circ(i^{H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*}

which implies that
i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}
induces isomorphism in homology for every
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
.

Now, we need to show that
D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}
is well-defined.

Let

H_{\eta}^{\prime},H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C,H_{\eta})

be two different Hamiltonians such that
H_{\eta}^{\prime}\leq H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime}
holds. Let
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
be arbitrary. From the previous lemma, we have a diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime*}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime*}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}^{\prime*}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}^{\prime*}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta}^{\prime}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime*},H_{\eta}^{\prime*}}_{L})_{*}}

which commutes. Since it holds that

i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime*},H_{\eta}^{*}}_{L}=i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime*},H_{\eta}^{\prime*}}_{L}\circ i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H_{\eta}^{*}}_{L},

we have that

D^{H^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}\circ(i^{H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*}=D^{H^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}\circ(i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime*},H_{\eta}^{\prime*}}_{L}\circ i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H_{\eta}^{*}}_{L})_{*}

=(D^{H^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}\circ(i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime*},H_{\eta}^{\prime*}}_{L})_{*})\circ(i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H_{\eta}^{*}}_{L})_{*}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}\circ(i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*}.

Let now
H_{\eta}^{\prime},H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},+}(C,H_{\eta})
be arbitrary. We can always find another perturbation
H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},+}(C,H_{\eta})
such that
H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime\prime}\leq H_{\eta}^{\prime},H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime}
. From the previous discussion, we have that

D^{H^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}\circ(i^{H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*}=D^{H^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}\circ(i^{H^{\prime\prime\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}\circ(i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*},\quad L\in(\eta,T_{\eta}).

This implies that

D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}\circ(i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H_{\eta}}_{L})_{*},\quad H^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C,H_{\eta})

is independent of
H^{\prime}_{\eta}
and therefore, well-defined. Moreover, from the fact that
D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}
and
(i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H_{\eta}}_{L})_{*}
are isomorphisms, it follows that
D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}
is an isomorphism.

Combining this diagram with the commutativity of the diagram from the previous lemma, i.e.,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime}_{\nu}}<L_{\nu}\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime}_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L_{\nu}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L_{\eta}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H^{\prime}_{\nu}}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}_{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H^{\prime}}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}

from the definitions of
D^{H_{\eta}}_{L_{\eta}}
and
D^{H_{\nu}}_{L_{\nu}}
, the conclusion follows.

∎

Positive
S^{1}
-equivariant symplectic homology

Let
H_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}_{MB}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
. We say that
H^{\prime}_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C)
is an
N
-admissible
S^{1}
-invariant perturbation of
H_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}_{MB}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
if

H^{\prime}_{\eta}(t,x,z)=H_{\eta}(x)+\delta\left(\beta(z)\sum_{S_{y}}\rho_{S_{y}}(x)f_{S_{y}}(t-\tau_{z},x)-\mu\rho(x)\widetilde{f}_{N}(z)\right).

Here,
\beta:S^{2N+1}\to\mathbb{R}
is an
S^{1}
-invariant cutoff function equal to
1
near
\operatorname{crit}(\widetilde{f}_{N})
and
0
outside of
U
, which is an
S^{1}
-invariant neighborhood of
\operatorname{crit}(\widetilde{f}_{N})
for which we have a pre-chosen local section. The function

\tau:U\to\mathbb{T},

is defined such that
\tau_{z}
is the time at which
\tau_{z}^{-1}z
belongs to the local section.

It holds that
\text{supp}(\rho_{S_{y}})\cap\overline{C}=\emptyset
and that

\text{supp}(\rho)\subset V,\quad\rho|_{B}=1,

where
B=\bigcup\text{supp}(\rho_{S_{y}})
and
V
is a bounded neighborhood of
B
such that
V\cap\overline{C}=\emptyset.

Moreover, we require the following conditions to hold:

(1)

\mu>0
is sufficiently large such that
H^{\prime}_{\eta}
is non-increasing along the gradient flow of
\widetilde{f}_{N}
.

(2)

\delta>0
is sufficiently small such that

•

H^{\prime}_{\eta}
is uniformly quadratically convex.

•

H_{\eta}+\delta\sum_{S_{y}}\rho_{S_{y}}f_{S_{y}}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
.

•

The set
P(H^{\prime}_{\eta},\widetilde{f}_{N})\setminus(\{0\}\times\operatorname{crit}(\widetilde{f}_{N}))
satisfies

\bigcup\left\{\theta\cdot(y_{\min},v)\mid\theta\in\mathbb{T},\ v\in\operatorname{crit}(\widetilde{f}_{N})\text{ and belongs to the local section}\right\}

\cup\bigcup\left\{\theta\cdot(y_{\max},v)\mid\theta\in\mathbb{T},\ v\in\operatorname{crit}(\widetilde{f}_{N})\text{ and belongs to the local section}\right\},

where
(y_{\min},y_{\max})
are critical pairs of
H_{\eta}+\delta\sum_{S_{y}}\rho_{S_{y}}f_{S_{y}}
.

•

For all
(y,v)\in P(H^{\prime}_{\eta},\widetilde{f}_{N})\setminus(\{0\}\times\operatorname{crit}(\widetilde{f}_{N}))
, we have

|\Phi_{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}(y,v)-T_{y}|<\min\left\{\frac{1}{\eta},\text{gap}(\eta)\right\},

•

\varepsilon<\Phi_{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}(y,v)<\eta
, for
(y,v)\in P(H^{\prime}_{\eta},\widetilde{f}_{N})\setminus(\{0\}\times\operatorname{crit}(\widetilde{f}_{N}))
.

As in the non-parameterized case, for all critical points
(0,v)\in\{0\}\times\operatorname{crit}(\widetilde{f}_{N})
, it holds that

\Phi_{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}(0,v)=\Phi_{H_{\eta}}(0)<\varepsilon,

since all perturbations are outside of
\overline{C}
.

For
H\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C)\subset\mathcal{H}_{\Theta}^{S^{1},N}(\widetilde{f}_{N},g_{N})
, we define the positive
S^{1}
-equivariant Floer complex denoted by

\{\operatorname{C}\operatorname{F}_{*}^{S^{1},N,+}(H),\partial^{F,S^{1}}\},

as the quotient complex

\operatorname{C}\operatorname{F}^{S^{1},N,+}(H)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\operatorname{C}\operatorname{F}^{S^{1},N}(H)/\operatorname{C}\operatorname{F}^{S^{1},N,<\varepsilon}(H).

This is well-defined since
\Phi_{H}
is non-increasing along trajectories. The filtration on this complex by
\Phi_{H}
is well-defined for the same reason. The homology of this complex is denoted by

\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}_{*}^{S^{1},N,+}(H,J).

This homology is independent of the choice of an almost complex structure.

Let
H_{\alpha}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N_{\alpha}}(C)
and
H_{\beta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N_{\beta}}(C)
be such that
(N_{\alpha},H_{\alpha})\leq(N_{\beta},H_{\beta})
.

Let
(H_{\alpha},J_{\alpha})
and
(H_{\beta},J_{\beta})
be such that the
S^{1}
-equivariant Floer complexes are well-defined.

Then a generic non-increasing homotopy
(H^{\alpha\beta}_{s},J^{\alpha\beta}_{s})
induces a chain map unique up to chain homotopy. This chain map induces a continuation map

i^{S^{1},+}_{H_{\beta},H_{\alpha}}:\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}^{S^{1},N_{\alpha},+}_{*}(H_{\alpha},J_{\alpha})\to\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}^{S^{1},N_{\beta},+}_{*}(H_{\beta},J_{\beta}),

which gives us a directed system. Here,
\alpha
and
\beta
do not represent the slope.

The set
\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C)
is cofinal in the family of
N
-admissible
S^{1}
-equivariant Hamiltonians (see
[
GH18
]
). Thus, we have

\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{S^{1},N,+,<L}(C)=\varinjlim_{H\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C)}\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}^{S^{1},N,+,<L}(H),

and

\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{S^{1},+,<L}(C)=\varinjlim_{H\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C),N\in\mathbb{N}}\operatorname{H}\operatorname{F}^{S^{1},+,<L}(H).

Since it is clear that

\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C)\subset\mathcal{H}^{S^{1},N}_{\Theta}(\widetilde{f}_{N},g_{N})
, for the
l
-reduced dual functional
\psi^{l}_{H^{*}}
associated with
H\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C)
, we can define positive
S^{1}
-equivariant Morse homology. Moreover, due to Theorem
8.2
, an analogous claim to the one from Remark
10.4
holds in the
S^{1}
-equivariant case. Thus, we can prove the next lemma in the same way as Lemma
10.7
.

Lemma 10.9
.

Let
C
be a non-degenerate, strongly convex domain whose interior contains the origin. Let
H_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C)
be arbitrary, where
\eta
is the slope at infinity and
T_{\eta}
is the minimal element of
\sigma(\partial C)
greater than
\eta
. The following holds:

(1)

For every
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
, there exists an isomorphism

D^{H_{\eta},S^{1},N}_{L}:\operatorname{H}^{S^{1}}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\to SH^{S^{1},N,+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2}).

(2)

This isomorphism is natural. Let
(N_{1},H_{\eta})\leq(N_{2},H_{\nu})
. For every
L_{\eta}\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L_{\nu}\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
such that
L_{\eta}\leq L_{\nu}
, the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{S^{1}}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<L_{\nu}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{S^{1},N_{2},+,<L_{\nu}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{S^{1}}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{S^{1},N_{1},+,<L_{\eta}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\nu},S^{1},N_{2}}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta},S^{1},N_{1}}_{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H,N_{2},N_{1}}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{S^{1},N_{2},N_{1},+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}

commutes.

Corollary 10.10
.

Let
C
be a non-degenerate, strongly convex domain whose interior contains the origin. Let
H_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}_{MB}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
be arbitrary, where
\eta
is the slope at infinity and
T_{\eta}
is the minimal element of
\sigma(\partial C)
greater than
\eta
. The following holds:

(1.1)

For every
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
, there exists an isomorphism

D^{H_{\eta},S^{1}}_{L}:\operatorname{H}^{S^{1}}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\to SH^{S^{1},+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2}).

(1.2)

This isomorphism is natural. Let
H_{\eta}\leq H_{\nu}
. Then for every
L_{\eta}\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L_{\nu}\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
such that
L_{\eta}\leq L_{\nu}
, the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{S^{1}}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<L_{\nu}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{S^{1},+,<L_{\nu}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{S^{1}}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{S^{1},+,<L_{\eta}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\nu},S^{1}}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta},S^{1}}_{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{S^{1},+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}

commutes.

Proof.

Let
H_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}_{MB}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
be arbitrary. Unlike in the case of the family
\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}(C)
, in
\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C)
we cannot find
H^{\prime}_{\eta}
such that

H_{\eta}\leq H^{\prime}_{\eta}.

On the other hand, we can always find
H^{\prime}_{\eta}
such that it is arbitrarily close to
H_{\eta}
and

H^{\prime}_{\eta}\leq H_{\eta}.

Hence, we denote by
\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C,H_{\eta})
the subset of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C)
consisting of
N
-admissible
S^{1}
-invariant perturbations
H^{\prime}_{\eta}
of
H_{\eta}
such that

•

H^{\prime}_{\eta}\leq H_{\eta}
.

From this, it follows that
\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}\leq\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}
, and therefore for
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
we have an inclusion

i^{H^{*}_{\eta},H^{\prime*}_{\eta},N}_{L}:(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\}\times S^{2N+1},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}\times S^{2N+1}).

We define

D^{H_{\eta},S^{1},N}_{L}:\operatorname{H}_{*}^{S^{1}}(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\}\times S^{2N+1},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}\times S^{2N+1})\to SH^{S^{1},N,+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})

as

D^{H_{\eta},S^{1},N}_{L}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta},S^{1},N}_{L}\circ(i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta},N}_{L})^{-1}_{*},\quad H^{\prime}_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F},S^{1},N}(C,H_{\eta}).

where
D^{H_{\eta},S^{1},N}
is the isomorphism from the previous lemma. Using analogous arguments as in Corollary
10.8
, we show that the definition of
D^{H_{\eta},S^{1},N}_{L}
is a well-defined isomorphism. Furthermore, if
H_{\eta}\leq H_{\nu}
and
N_{1}\leq N_{2}
, we have that the following diagram

(10.11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{S^{1}}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<L_{\nu}\}\times S^{2N_{2}+1},\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\}\times S^{2N_{2}+1};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{S^{1},N_{2},+,<L_{\nu}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}^{S^{1}}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<L_{\eta}\}\times S^{2N_{1}+1},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}\times S^{2N_{1}+1};\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{S^{1},N_{1},+,<L_{\eta}}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\nu},S^{1},N_{2}}_{L_{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta},S^{1},N_{1}}_{L_{\eta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H,N_{2},N_{1}}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{S^{1},N_{2},N_{1},+}_{L_{\nu},L_{\eta}}}

commutes for
L_{\eta}\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L_{\nu}\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
such that
L_{\eta}\leq L_{\nu}
. Taking
H_{\eta}=H_{\nu}
and
L_{\eta}=L_{\nu}
in the commutative diagram (
10.11
), we prove claim (1) by taking a direct limit. On the other hand, by taking the direct limit of the same diagram for
H_{\eta}
and
H_{\nu}
, we prove claim (2).

∎

Proof of Proposition
10.2
.

For
H_{\eta}=\varphi_{\eta}\circ H_{C}
where
\varphi_{\eta}\in\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
, we define the set

\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}_{MB}(C;H_{\eta})

as the set of all
H^{\prime}_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}_{MB}(C)
with the slope
\eta
such that

•

H_{\eta}\leq H^{\prime}_{\eta}
.

Then we have the inclusion

i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}:(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})\to(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}),\quad L\in(\eta,T_{\eta}).

This inclusion induces an isomorphism in homology. Indeed, we can find
H^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}_{MB}(C)
such that
H^{\prime\prime}_{\eta}\leq H_{\eta}
and
H^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\eta}=\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\eta}\circ H_{C}
, where
\varphi^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\eta}\in\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
is such that
H_{\eta}^{\prime}\leq H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

From this, we have the following factorizations:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{H_{\eta}^{*},H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta}}_{L}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{\Psi_{H^{\prime\prime\prime*}_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{\prime\prime\prime*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime\prime*},H^{\prime*}_{\eta}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime\prime*},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}}

From Corollary
10.8
and Proposition
9.5
, we have that
i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime*},H^{\prime\prime*}_{\eta}}_{L}
and
i^{H_{\eta}^{\prime\prime\prime*},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}
induce isomorphisms in homology. Therefore,
i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}
induces an isomorphism in homology.

For every
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
, we define

D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}:\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H^{*}_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\};\mathbb{Z}_{2})\to SH^{+,<L}_{*+n}(C;\mathbb{Z}_{2})

as

D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}=D^{H^{\prime}_{\eta}}_{L}\circ(i^{H^{\prime*}_{\eta},H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L})_{*},\quad H^{\prime}_{\eta}\in\mathcal{H}^{\mathcal{F}}_{MB}(C;H_{\eta}).

To show that this is indeed a well-defined natural isomorphism, we use similar arguments as in the proof of Corollary
10.8
.

∎

We prove the
S^{1}
-equivariant case by using Corollary
10.10
and Remark
9.6
in the same way. This justifies Remark
10.3
.

11  Positive (
S^{1}
-equivariant) symplectic homology and Clarke’s duality

In this section, we will prove theorems
C.1
and
C.2
. First, we extract important properties of the family
\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
.

(1)

For every
L\notin\sigma(\partial C)
such that
L>\min\sigma(\partial C)
, there exists
\varphi_{\eta}\in\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
such that
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
.

(2)

For every
L\notin\sigma(\partial C)
such that
L>\min\sigma(\partial C)
and every two functions
\varphi_{\eta},\varphi_{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
such that
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
, there exists
\varphi_{\mu}\in\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
such that
\varphi_{\eta},\varphi_{\nu}\leq\varphi_{\mu}
and
L\in(\mu,T_{\mu})
.

(3)

For every
\min\sigma(\partial C)<L_{1}<L_{2}
which do not belong to the spectrum of
\partial C
, there exist
\varphi_{\eta},\varphi_{\nu}\in\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
such that
\varphi_{\eta}\leq\varphi_{\nu}
with
L_{1}\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L_{2}\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
.

Proof of Theorem
C.1
.

We define

D_{L}:\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{C}<L\})\to S\operatorname{H}^{+,<L}_{*+n+1}(C),\quad L\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\sigma(\partial C),

in the following way:

D_{L}=D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}\circ D^{H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}

where
L\notin\sigma(\partial C)
and
L>\min\sigma(\partial C)
. Hamiltonian
H_{\eta}=\varphi_{\eta}\circ H_{C}
is such that
\varphi_{\eta}\in\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
and
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
. Maps
D^{H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}
and
D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}
are isomorphisms from propositions
9.5
and
10.2
respectively (Note:
D^{H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}
is well-defined since
\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)\subset\mathcal{F}^{*}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
by Remark
10.1
).
For
L<\min\sigma(\partial C)
, there exists a unique isomorphism

D_{L}=0,

since both homologies are zero.

From property (1) of the family
\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
, we have that we can always find such
H_{\eta}
from the definition of
D_{L}
.

On the other hand, if
H_{\eta}\leq H_{\nu}
are such that
L\in(\eta,T_{\eta})
and
L\in(\nu,T_{\nu})
, we have commuting diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{C}<L\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*+1}(\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}^{*}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}^{*}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{C}<L\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*+1}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}^{*}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}^{*}}<\varepsilon\})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H^{*}_{\nu}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H}_{L})_{*}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*+1}(\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L}_{*+n+1}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*+1}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<L\},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L}_{*+n+1}(C)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\nu}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H}_{L})_{*}}

Combining these two diagrams, we have

D^{H_{\nu}}_{L}\circ D^{H^{*}_{\nu}}_{L}=D^{H_{\nu}}_{L}\circ(i^{H}_{L})_{*}\circ D^{H^{*}_{\eta}}=D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}\circ D^{H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}.

Therefore, due to property (2) of the family
\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
, we conclude that the definition of
D_{L}
is independent of
H_{\eta}
, which means it is well-defined. Since
D^{H_{\eta}}_{L}
and
D^{H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L}
are isomorphisms,
D_{L}
is an isomorphism.

Let
\min\sigma(\partial C)<L_{1}<L_{2}
be any numbers that do not belong to the spectrum. Let
H_{\eta}=\varphi_{\eta}\circ H_{C}
and
H_{\nu}=\varphi_{\nu}\circ H_{C}
be Hamiltonians such that
\varphi_{\eta}
and
\varphi_{\nu}
are from property (3) of the family
\mathcal{F}_{\operatorname{lin}}(C)
. Then from propositions
9.5
and
10.2
, we have the following diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{C}<L_{2}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*+1}(\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}^{*}}<L_{2}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}^{*}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{C}<L_{1}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*+1}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}^{*}}<L_{1}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}^{*}}<\varepsilon\})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H^{*}_{\nu}}_{L_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H^{*}_{\eta}}_{L_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{C}_{L_{2},L_{1}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H}_{L_{2},L_{1}})_{*}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*+1}(\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<L_{2}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\nu}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L_{2}}_{*+n+1}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*+1}(\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<L_{1}\},\{\Psi_{H_{\eta}}<\varepsilon\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces SH^{+,<L_{1}}_{*+n+1}(C)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\nu}}_{L_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{H_{\eta}}_{L_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{H}_{L_{2},L_{1}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{+}_{L_{2},L_{1}}}

From these two diagrams and the definition of
D_{L_{1}}
and
D_{L_{2}}
, we have that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{C}<L_{2}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\operatorname{H}^{+,<L_{2}}_{*+n+1}(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{H}_{*}(\{\Psi_{C}<L_{1}\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\operatorname{H}^{+,<L_{1}}_{*+n+1}(C)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{L_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{C}_{L_{2},L_{1}})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{L_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{+}_{L_{2},L_{1}}}

commutes. If
L_{1}<\min\sigma(\partial C)
, commutativity of the previous diagram is trivial since
D_{L_{1}}=0
.

∎

Since propositions
9.5
and
10.2
hold in the
S^{1}
-equivariant case (see Remarks
9.6
and
10.3
), the proof of Theorem
C.2
is the same.

Remark 11.1
.

The isomorphism from Theorem
C.2
induces an isomorphism at the limit:

D^{S^{1}}:H_{*}^{S^{1}}(\Lambda)\to\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{S^{1},+}_{*+n+1}(C).

Indeed, since we can understand
H_{*}^{S^{1}}(\Lambda)
as the direct limit over
L
of
H_{*}^{S^{1}}(\{\Psi_{C}<L\})
, this isomorphism is defined as

D^{S^{1}}(x)=i^{S^{1},+}_{L}\circ D^{S^{1}}_{L}(x),\quad x\in H_{*}^{S^{1}}(\{\Psi_{C}<L\}),

where

i_{L}^{S^{1},+}:\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{S^{1},+,<L}_{*+n+1}(W;\mathbb{F})\to\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{S^{1},+}_{*+n+1}(W;\mathbb{F}),

is the inclusion. This isomorphism is natural, i.e., it holds that
i_{L}^{S^{1},+}\circ D^{S^{1}}_{L}=D^{S^{1}}\circ(i^{C}_{L})_{*}
where

i^{C}_{L}:\{\Psi_{C}<L\}\to\Lambda

is the inclusion. The same conclusion holds for the isomorphism from Theorem
C.1
.

12  Gutt-Hutchings capacities and Ekeland-Hofer spectral invariants

In this section, we will prove Theorem
A
. In the paper
[
EH87
]
, Ekeland and Hofer introduced spectral invariants using Clarke’s dual functional and the Fadell-Rabinowitz index. Both the spectral invariants and the Fadell-Rabinowitz
1
1

1

In this definition, the Fadell-Rabinowitz index is shifted by 1, aligning with the definition provided in
[
FR78
, Section 5]
.
index were defined in the introduction. We recall the definition of the spectral invariants.

Ekeland-Hofer spectral invariants:

Let
C_{0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2n}
be a bounded convex domain whose interior contains the origin. In Section
9
, we introduced Clarke’s dual functional

\Psi_{C_{0}}:\Lambda\to\mathbb{R},\quad\Psi_{C_{0}}(x)=\int\limits_{\mathbb{T}}\operatorname{H}^{*}_{C_{0}}(-J_{0}\dot{x}(t))\,dt.

Now let
C\subset\mathbb{R}^{2n}
be a bounded convex domain. We define the
k
-th Ekeland–Hofer spectral invariant as

s_{k}(C;\mathbb{F})=\inf\{L>0\mid\operatorname{ind}_{\operatorname{FR}}(\{\Psi_{C_{0}}<L\};\mathbb{F})\geq k\},\quad k\geq 1,

where
C_{0}
is any translation of
C
whose interior contains the origin. This definition does not depend on translation, and therefore the sequence of numbers
(s_{k}(C;\mathbb{F}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is well-defined.

This sequence enjoys the following properties.

•

Systole
:
s_{1}(C)=\min\Psi_{C}=\min\sigma(\partial C)
.

•

Monotonicity:
The sequence
(s_{k}(C))_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is increasing with respect to
k
.

•

Spectrality
: Every
s_{k}(C)
is a critical value of
\Psi_{C}
and therefore an element of
\sigma(\partial C)
.

•

Hausdorff-continuity
: For any sequence of bounded convex domains
C_{i}
that converges to a bounded convex domain
C
in the Hausdorff distance topology, it holds that
s_{k}(C_{i})\to s_{k}(C)
.

Lemma 12.1
.

Let
\mathbb{F}
be a field. Then

s_{k}(C;\mathbb{F})=\inf\{L>0\mid(i^{C}_{L})_{*}\neq 0\},

where
(i^{C}_{L})_{*}:\operatorname{H}_{2k-2}^{S^{1}}(\{\Psi_{C_{0}}<L;\mathbb{F}\})\to\operatorname{H}_{2k-2}^{S^{1}}(\Lambda;\mathbb{F}).

Proof.

We have that

s_{k}(C)=\inf\{L>0\mid\operatorname{ind}_{\operatorname{FR}}(\{\Psi_{C}<L\})\geq k\},

where
\operatorname{ind}_{\operatorname{FR}}(\{\Psi_{C}<L\})=\sup_{k\geq 0}\{k+1\mid(\pi^{L}_{2})^{*}e^{k}=0\}
and

\pi^{L}_{2}:\{\Psi_{C}<L\}\times_{S^{1}}ES^{1}\to BS^{1},\quad\pi^{L}_{2}([x,y])=[y]

is the quotient projection. Since we have the following commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}_{S^{1}}(\Lambda)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}_{S^{1}}(\Psi_{C}<L)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}_{S^{1}}(BS^{1}),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i^{C}_{L})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi^{L}_{2})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{2})^{*}}

and
(\pi_{2})^{*}
is a ring isomorphism (since
\Lambda
is
S^{1}
-contractible), we conclude that
s_{k}(C)=\inf\{L>0\mid(i^{C}_{L})^{*}\neq 0\}
, where
(i^{C}_{L})^{*}:\operatorname{H}_{S^{1}}^{2k-2}(\Lambda)\to\operatorname{H}_{S^{1}}^{2k-2}(\{\Psi_{C}<L\}).
Since
\mathbb{F}
is a field, by the universal coefficients theorem, we conclude that the claim holds.
∎

Gutt-Hutchings capacities:

In
[
GH18
]
, Gutt and Hutchings extracted a sequence of capacities from positive
S^{1}
-equivariant homology. Let
W
be a nice star-shaped domain, meaning the closure of a bounded open star-shaped set with a smooth boundary such that
\partial W
is transverse to the radial vector field. By
[
GH18
, Remark 4.8]
, in this case we have

c_{k}^{GH}(W;\mathbb{F})=\inf\{L>0\mid i_{L}^{S^{1},+}\neq 0\},

where
i_{L}^{S^{1},+}:\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{S^{1},+,<L}_{n-1+2k}(W;\mathbb{F})\to\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{S^{1},+}_{n-1+2k}(W;\mathbb{F}).
Here,
\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{S^{1},+,<L}_{n-1+2k}(W;\mathbb{F})
denotes the filtered positive
S^{1}
-equivariant homology, while the group
\operatorname{S}\operatorname{H}^{S^{1},+}_{n-1+2k}(W;\mathbb{F})
is the full positive
S^{1}
-equivariant homology. The map
i_{L}^{S^{1},+}
refers to the inclusion map. These capacities are extended continuously to bounded star-shaped domains that are not nice.

We list some of the properties of these capacities.

•

Monotonicity:
The sequence
(c^{GH}_{k}(W))_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is monotone with respect to
k
.

•

Spectrality:
Every
c^{GH}_{k}(W)
is an element of
\sigma(\partial W)
.

•

Hausdorff-continuity:
For any sequence of bounded star-shaped domains
W_{i}
that converges to a bounded star-shaped domain
W
in the Hausdorff distance topology, it holds that
c^{GH}_{k}(W_{i})\to c^{GH}_{k}(W)
.

Moreover, being that they are capacities these invariants are monotonic with respect to symplectic embeddings. See
[
GH18
]
for the definition of these capacities for general Liouville domains.

Proof of Theorem
A
.

Let
C\subset\mathbb{R}^{2n}
be a non-degenerate, strongly convex domain. By translation, we can assume that its interior contains the origin.

By Theorem
C.2
and Remark
11.1
, we have

\displaystyle c^{GH}_{k}(C)

\displaystyle=\inf\limits\{L>0\mid i_{L}^{S^{1},+}\neq 0\}=\inf\{L>0\mid(D^{S^{1}})^{-1}\circ i_{L}^{S^{1},+}\circ D_{L}^{S^{1}}\neq 0\}

\displaystyle=\inf\{L>0\mid(D^{S^{1}})^{-1}\circ D^{S^{1}}\circ(i_{L}^{C})_{*}\neq 0\}=\inf\{L>0\mid(i^{C}_{L})_{*}\neq 0\}=s_{k}(C).

The last equality follows from Lemma
12.1
. Since both the Gutt-Hutchings capacities and the spectral invariants are continuous with respect to the Hausdorff distance topology, and non-degenerate strongly convex domains are dense in bounded convex domains, the theorem follows.
∎
